# nginx converter status

## CURRENT

- Draft PR #469 fixed-upload HEAD success-boundary differential review after
  rebasing onto merged #475/#478. The unchanged pinned nginx 1.29.7 versus
  converter-generated ordinary-RUT test passes 3/3 with its exact split-upload,
  upstream-wire, response, retirement and no-retry gates intact. This is
  nginx.conf translation evidence, never direct nginx.conf runtime support, and
  no compatibility status changes are claimed before review and full CI
  complete.

## NEXT

1. Independently review the rebased unchanged differential and its evidence.
2. Run full CI and merge PR #469 only after review remains green.
3. Update the compatibility matrix and issue cross-links only after merge/full
   CI; keep #468/#270 open and `proxy_read_timeout` PARTIAL meanwhile.
4. Investigate the minimal precise-timer design for the existing
   `BodylessNonHeadContentLengthZero` GET/CL0 profile, first pinning nginx's
   incomplete-response-header timing origin instead of assuming refresh.
5. Investigate the precise-timer design for
   `FixedContentLengthUploadHeaderOnlyHead` zero-origin-response expiry after
   upload completion. Both investigations remain under #268 unless a distinct
   minimal RUT capability gap is found.

## BACKLOG

- Account for the reproducible one-byte-per-verification LLVM
  `LLVMVerifyModule` LeakSanitizer allocation in JIT loader tests: #304. Existing
  and new loader cases reproduce it; it is tooling evidence, not a #303 semantic
  blocker or owned-policy leak.
- General prefix, exact, `^~`, regex, and nested location selection.
- Multiple servers, listen/default-server selection, and `server_name`.
- Pinned nginx semantics for absolute-form targets; generated programs now route
  a miss to the bounded ANY fail-closed 400, which is not an equivalence claim.
- Variables, rewrite phases, internal redirects, upstream groups, TLS, static
  files, error pages, and logging compatibility.
- Make the complete historical frontend test process leak-clean under LSan:
  #315. Exact allocation accounting attributes the current full-suite failure
  to unchanged old tests; #314's focused acceptance paths are leak-clean.

## BLOCKED

- #468/#270 remain open and PARTIAL pending independent review, full CI and
  merge of the rebased fixed-upload HEAD success-boundary differential in Draft
  PR #469. Its upload gating, exact binary body, upstream wire, response,
  downstream keep-alive, origin retirement, no-retry, access-log and cleanup
  checks must remain strict.
- broader nginx default upstream request behavior: #252.
- broader nginx default proxied response behavior and differential evidence: #253.
- upstream invalid-status/HTTP/0.9 fallback semantics: #266.
- timeout-cause-specific forward failure policy selection: #267. The discovered
  SocketCreate, ConnectSubmit, and asynchronous ConnectCompletion configured-502
  paths and sequential ordinary-RUT unavailable-upstream production wire are
  implemented; broader issue scope remains tracked separately.
- exact escaped-byte syntax for ordinary local-response bodies: #279. String
  response bodies retain `\\n` as two literal bytes while failure-policy byte
  strings decode escapes. This does not block #276, whose static body is arbitrary.
- ordinary local-response HEAD body suppression: #281. This is separate from
  #280 persistence and does not block the current H1.1 GET production slice.
- ordinary local-response HTTP/1.0 status-line fidelity: #282. #280 may correct
  H1.0 connection lifecycle, but full HTTP/1.0 wire equivalence remains blocked.
- ordinary local-response 205 no-content framing: #283. This is separate from
  #280 and excluded from the current 200 GET slice.
- global proxy request Transfer-Encoding validation: #284. #280 classifies
  ambiguous framing conservatively for local-response reuse only; faithful proxy
  rejection/forwarding and nginx differential evidence remain separate.
- broader response-read inactivity semantics: #268/#270 remain open. The narrow
  cleartext H1.1 bodyless `HeaderOnlyHead` + converter-required
  `Http11FixedStrip`, fresh-single-IPv4-upstream, explicit-downstream-close
  profile now has live precise timing and pinned-nginx phase evidence through
  #460/#463/#462. PR #465 extends the same runtime primitive to default
  downstream keep-alive and already contains the public ordinary-RUT
  two-request behavior proof; PR #466 makes its generated buffering `None`
  provenance explicit. PR #467 adds the matching converter differential with
  four simultaneously held ports and independent side resources. The pinned
  nginx configuration omits `proxy_buffering` and
  therefore uses nginx's default buffering; the generated ordinary-RUT response
  policy uses buffering `None`. Broader methods, request/response bodies,
  upstream reuse/retry, TLS/H2/epoll and wider lifecycle acceptance remain
  unproven and must continue to reject outside declared converter bounds.
- broader proxied response buffering and downstream commit semantics remain open
  under #271, including bodyful/default-buffering response commit semantics.
  PR #450/#451 cover only the bounded generated ordinary-RUT response-buffering
  `None` HEAD profile and its public ordinary-source evidence.
- nginx `proxy_read_timeout` remains `PARTIAL` under #270. Parser recognition and
  bounded lowering exist, and the exact narrow HEAD profile above is now proven;
  that evidence cannot be generalized to the broader #268 domain or to nginx's
  default/bodyful buffering semantics in #271. No silent policy omission or
  approximate lowering is permitted.
- #271 remains blocked for bodyful/default-buffering response commit semantics;
  PR #450/#451 cover only the generated ordinary-RUT response-buffering `None`
  positive-CL HEAD profile.
- broader reusable keep-alive `HEAD` failures: timeout behavior and the
  remaining #253 response semantics still depend on #265; invalid-status
  HTTP/0.9 fallback is separately #266. The epoll prerequisite #262 and
  recv-retirement capability #264 are closed. The incomplete-EOF converter
  differential is complete.

## RECENTLY COMPLETED

- PR #478 (merge `d8aa9c9e`) closes #477/#479/#481/#482 after repairing the
  exact capacity/source validators and two general request-state ownership
  boundaries exposed by #475. Its final seven stale-oracle repairs preserve the
  new request-framing selector while keeping predecessor attempts, request-
  policy materialization scratch and local successor state request-scoped.
  Independent review approved and all 15 applicable CI jobs passed; #480 closed
  after its unchanged keep-alive differential passed repeatedly.

- PR #475 merged at `b3f18925` and adds the bounded root-timeout exact-HEAD
  runtime framing selection to generated ordinary RUT: requests with a
  Content-Length select ID2 while requests without one retain ID1, with one
  identical timeout bundle and public compiler traversal. The follow-up exact
  capacity/source and request-state regressions are resolved by merged PR #478;
  this entry alone is not fixed-upload nginx differential evidence.

- PR #476 (merge `d7f5e529`) closes #474 by raising the general allocation-free
  public lexer capacity exactly to 932 tokens, derived from the maximum current
  HTTP-profile converter output (931 lexical tokens plus EOF). Exact success,
  EOF-push failure, token-push failure and atomic loader-publication boundaries
  are covered without grammar/runtime expansion. Independent review approved;
  all 15 applicable CI jobs passed and the privileged job skipped as expected.

- PR #473 (merge `5e0fc843`, accepted head `59567a4b`) closes #472 by adding a
  dedicated verified-only exact-HEAD framing-selection preflight. The compiler
  proves the two-branch CFG and shared timeout profile, while runtime dispatch
  revalidates framing, policy, bundle and state before upstream effects and
  closes/reclaims on mismatch. Independent review approved; all 15 applicable
  CI jobs passed and the privileged job skipped as expected.

- PR #471 (merge `99281448`, accepted head `f8318545`) admits policy ID2 only
  to the bounded positive fixed-upload HEAD response-read-timeout profile. A
  profile-specific closed set is revalidated across analyzer, MIR, RIR,
  verifier and runtime ownership/materialization, with public ordinary-RUT
  HEAD/ANY evidence and exact upload/wire/200/504/502 proof. Independent review
  approved and all 15 applicable CI jobs passed. Converter lowering was added
  subsequently by #475; nginx compatibility remains PARTIAL.

- PR #470 (merge `ca4c6049`, accepted head `9a0d0edf`) adds the explicit
  ordinary-RUT `content_length_position: "after_host"` request-policy variant.
  It preserves immutable ID0/ID1 wires, emits a validated positive reconstructed
  Content-Length immediately after rewritten Host, does not inject a missing
  Content-Length, and fails closed for CL0 and unsupported framing before
  upstream effects. Independent review approved; focused tests and 13 CI jobs
  passed, while the remaining clang Release runner was still stuck in external
  tool installation at merge. The policy remains excluded from all timeout and
  buffering profiles except through the subsequently reviewed bounded
  fixed-upload HEAD admission and framing-selection path.

- PR #467 (merge `c08a4d6a`) adds the pinned-nginx 1.29.7 versus
  converter-generated ordinary-RUT
  differential for the bounded default-downstream-keep-alive bodyless H1.1 HEAD
  `proxy_read_timeout 1s` profile. Both sides receive an incomplete origin
  response header about 400ms after request completion, retain the initial
  response-header deadline, emit the exact header-only keep-alive 504 at about
  one second, retire origin 1 without retry, and serve request 2 on the same
  downstream socket through a fresh origin with the exact HEAD 200 close/EOF
  control. The generated side proves public CLI O2/JIT/io_uring execution,
  fixed HTTP/1.1 upstream request policy and response buffering `None`; nginx
  retains its omitted-`proxy_buffering` default. Independent review approved and
  final CI passed all 15 applicable jobs. #268/#270/#271 remain open and
  `proxy_read_timeout` remains `PARTIAL`.

- PR #465 (merge `3bcca79b`) extends the generation-owned precise response-read
  timer to the bounded default-downstream-keep-alive bodyless H1.1 HEAD runtime
  profile. It preserves the pinned #461 initial response-header deadline, exact
  configured keep-alive 504 settlement, origin retirement and successor
  isolation. Its strengthened tests distinguish that origin from both an
  incorrect fragment-reset timer and the old approximately two-second
  TimerWheel path. Independent review approved and the final CI passed all 15
  applicable jobs. The strengthened existing ordinary-source test also proves
  configured keep-alive 504/origin retirement followed by a second request over
  the retained downstream socket and a fresh origin; PR #466 explicitly pins
  its RIR/config buffering `None` provenance. #268/#270 remain open and
  `PARTIAL` pending the converter differential.

- PR #462 (merge `46815019`) completes the real pinned-nginx 1.29.7 versus
  converter-generated ordinary-RUT phase differential for the narrow cleartext
  H1.1 bodyless HEAD, explicit downstream-close profile. The pinned nginx
  configuration omits `proxy_buffering` and uses nginx's default buffering;
  the generated ordinary-RUT response policy uses buffering `None`. Zero
  progress measures about 1.00140s/1.00082s (nginx/RUT), and delayed incomplete
  response headers about 1.00145s/1.00081s from complete upstream request; both
  match the exact Date-normalized header-only 504/zero-body/EOF. The complete
  HEAD response matches the exact 200 control before the timeout floor. Every
  phase proves one exact upstream episode/no retry and only declared origin
  sends. `proxy_read_timeout` remains `PARTIAL` because #268/#270/#271 are broader.

- PR #463 (merge `3d009441`) closes #461 for the same bounded profile. Accepted

- #268/#271 positive-body OPTIONS capability design audit at `f421bd9c` finds that
  existing generic RUT machinery is sufficient: exact OPTIONS and method-omitted ANY
  route identities, FixedStrip fixed-upload proof, CompleteContentLength buffering,
  per-forward deadline progress and retirement already compose without a new public
  primitive, state field, ABI or compiler change. The minimum implementation widens
  only the central method-by-buffering cell for OPTIONS+CompleteContentLength and
  adds an OPTIONS-only fixed-upload shape guard rejecting Max-Forwards, Origin and
  all case-insensitive Access-Control-* headers. None/invalid buffering, OPTIONS-star,
  CL0/bodyless independent paths, invalid framing, explicit connection intent and
  all other method/protocol/transport domains stay fail-closed. Required tests cover
  exact/ANY positives, streaming/shape negatives, method-by-buffering truth table,
  partial upload/reload, route/policy/handler/config/episode ownership, first-batch,
  prebuilt D2 and transactional forgeries. No new issue is needed; public wire and
  converter lowering remain later separate tasks.

- #268/#271 pinned nginx positive-CL origin-form OPTIONS semantics are recorded by
  PR #436 at merge `60c69971` (accepted head `ce9cafc5`). One independent nginx-only
  1.29.7 oracle uses a root loopback proxy with `proxy_read_timeout 1s` and omits
  request/response buffering controls, proxy HTTP version and set-header directives.
  A valid octet-stream CL12 binary OPTIONS request arrives 5+7 with at least 1.2s
  between fragments; nginx keeps the client open and quiet and makes zero origin
  connections until the body completes, then emits one byte-exact method/target/
  query/Host/CL/X-Test/Content-Type request and binary body. Four permit-gated CL12
  response writes have 350–650ms gaps and span over 1s; nginx publishes no response
  before completion, then emits the exact Date-normalized keep-alive response and
  retires the sole origin with no retry. OPTIONS-star, Max-Forwards and CORS remain
  excluded. Different-Luna review approved; Sol's focused required-mode CTest passes
  1/1 in 3.65s, and exact-head CI run `33716493332` passes 15/15. No RUT or converter
  code changed; positive-body OPTIONS admission and #270 lowering remain open.

  DELETE as the next smallest RUT gap. Frontend/RIR, route matching and bodyless

  Existing upload, rewrite, buffering and timer primitives are method-independent,

- #273 atomic RouteConfig ownership/runtime activation landed in `aafa6744`
  after one independent `REQUEST_CHANGES` and final `APPROVE`. Bounded generic
  policies are deep-owned with semantic dedup/remap and transactional production
  install; configured/partial hand-built and reload metadata can no longer be
  silently ignored. Strict-parsed HTTP/1.1 route misses select exact method then
  ANY after normal routes; malformed/recovered/HTTP1.0/invalid states close with

  remain unchanged. Review found two blockers: source-pointer rebase still used
  native subtraction after integer validation, and H2 tests stopped before the
  outer close path. Fixes use checked integer offsets before any write and a real
  `on_h2_data` hit→miss regression proving zero sends and balanced cleanup. Both
  implementation and review passed normal plus ASan/UBSan focused coverage; Sol
  passed full Network, Http2Conn, RouteArt, and serve-loader 4/4. No public wire
  or converter differential landed, so #273/#271/#270 remain open and the matrix
  rows stay `BLOCKED_BY_RUT`.
- #273 guarded ownership-only attempt was rejected before commit and fully
  removed. Although its deep-copy/dedup/atomic-table tests passed, independent
  review found a blocking activation bypass: public RouteConfig builders or
  fields could be installed through hand-built config/`Shard::reload_config`
  without passing `populate_route_config`; runtime would ignore the successful
  metadata and silently return legacy unmatched 200. The compiler population
  guard alone was therefore insufficient. Sol chose the already approved design
  fallback: do not scatter publication guards or expose half-active APIs; merge
  ownership with H1 dispatch, configured-H2 fail-close, and guard removal as one
  atomic increment. Luna removed only the uncommitted phase-2 patch via
  `apply_patch`; status/diff are clean and no phase-3 code was introduced.
- #273 compiler foundation landed in `1fe16907` after one independent
  `REQUEST_CHANGES` round and final `APPROVE`. Generic bounded 400..599 strict
  local-response policies and main-source `unmatched [METHOD]` declarations now
  parse with contextual CONNECT/TRACE, enforce exact fields/bytes/HEAD rules,
  and propagate through AST/HIR/MIR/RIR with owned bytes, deterministic safe
  printing, and forged table/ID/pointer/enum/capacity rejection. Review found
  that the first printer trusted forged counts/pointers and that copy/move and
  transaction coverage was incomplete; the fix validates before any byte read,
  emits one invalid-table marker, rebases empty/binary bodies across all four
  copy/move forms, and rolls back body/policy/method IDs on malformed escapes or
  later parse failure. Most importantly, `populate_route_config` rejects a
  nonzero count or any nonzero fixed method ID before verification or mutation;
  valid source fails loader at Register and omitted source remains unchanged.
  Sol passed full RIR, Frontend, and serve-loader suites 3/3 in 70.77s after the
  independent review. No RouteConfig ownership/runtime selection/converter
  behavior landed, so #273/#271/#270 remain open and both rows stay blocked.
- #273 general RUT capability design completed after two independent
  `REQUEST_CHANGES` rounds and final `APPROVE`. The selected bounded primitive is
  a per-program method-keyed unmatched table referencing generic strict local-
  response policies; matched routes win, then strict-parsed HTTP/1.1 misses use
  exact method before ANY. Policies are bounded, deep-owned, status 400..599,
  and carry current Date, request-derived persistence, HEAD disposition, and
  body bytes. Malformed/recovered and HTTP/1.0 configured misses close with zero
  bytes; unread bodies force close; configured H2 misses protocol-close without
  legacy 200 or H1 bytes. Omitted config preserves legacy H1/H2 200. CONNECT and
  TRACE are contextual selectors; declarations are main-source/program scoped.
  CONNECT origin-form and OPTIONS absolute-form remain outside compatibility
  claims. Most importantly, compiler/config foundation phases retain a
  production activation guard rejecting nonzero tables before mutation; it may
  be removed only atomically with H1 dispatch and H2 fail-close. Issue #273 body
  now contains the reviewed acceptance criteria. No implementation landed yet.

  Converter behavior is unchanged and #268/#271/#270 stay open.
- Post-wire feasibility review concluded `NO`: no currently accepted nginx.conf
  slice can truthfully lower default buffering for its entire admitted domain.
  The converter emits one method-omitted route with a HEAD conditional; exact GET
  plus ANY fallback splitting is mechanically possible but would silently stream
  or fail closed for body-bearing GET, HEAD, write/other methods, and broader
  response shapes. Omitted nginx timeout also means 60s, whereas current lowering
  emits neither deadline nor buffering. No new issue is needed: #271 covers the
  buffering method/body/response gap, #268 the inactivity lifecycle breadth, and
  #270 the dependent explicit/default lowering. The selected next design extends
  the existing barrier to #268's bodyless known non-HEAD exact/ANY method identity;
  request bodies, HEAD, broader framing/status/size, and converter differential
  remain blocked afterward.

- #268 general RUT design investigation and independent `APPROVE_DESIGN`:
  `response_read_timeout` is a per-forward upstream response-read inactivity
  interval, not a total request deadline. Exact duration literals representing
  1..63 whole seconds fit the current wheel; zero is absence and explicit
  values never inherit the global 30-second timeout. The scalar extends the
  existing policy bundle without changing the packed handler ABI. Runtime work
  must pin request/config/episode ownership, refresh only on exact positive
  upstream progress, settle same-batch Recv/Timeout races explicitly, and keep
  every unproven phase zero-byte fail-closed. Converter lowering remains later.

  rendezvous: finish request-1 bookkeeping once, park all request-2 parse/route/
  rearm work while retirement is active, preserve positive downstream bytes,
  close on EOF/error, and resume once after the current CQE batch. An inactive
  tombstone may pass only the exact current successor token while continuing to
  swallow retired/malformed tokens without accounting. C2 deliberately cannot
  clear/overwrite the tombstone, start a second strict retirement, enable reusable
  HEAD, or alter nginx converter/source/compiler behavior; those remain later
  reviewed batons.

## OPEN NGINX COMPATIBILITY ISSUES

- #373 — one literal `proxy_hide_header X-Compat-Hidden` on the minimal root
  proxy. Pinned semantics, parser/model and generic ordinary-RUT lowering are
  complete; final four-side generated behavior evidence is CURRENT. Broader
  response policy remains #253.

## OPEN RUT CAPABILITY ISSUES
- #252 — upstream HTTP request policy for `forward`.
- #253 — proxied response header policy.
- #265 — all-upstream-I/O retirement before reusable failure responses.
- #266 — upstream HTTP/0.9/status-line-less close-delimited response semantics.
- #352 — preserve and expose bounded downstream Host plus ordered actual upstream
  endpoint/status history in generic access records. Current differentials prove
  these facts independently and do not claim they exist in access text.

## KNOWN SEMANTIC RISKS

- Closed #347 makes generic RUT access `req_size` preserve the downstream HTTP/1
  request independently from rebuilt upstream wire length. The nginx converter
  still does not parse or lower `log_format`/`access_log` `$request_length`; #362
  owns that compatibility work and #363 blocks lowering on a generic source-level
  sink/format/publication capability split into #364/#365/#366. Current periodic
  rings silently drop on fullness and acknowledge neither write success nor
  failure, so only #366 can close the live-publication gap. No row may claim
  nginx access-format support.
- Current RUT access text does not expose downstream Host or actual upstream
  endpoint/status history. #352 tracks the generic capability; tests may use
  request/backend/downstream observations but must not call those access fields.
- nginx version must be pinned in differential evidence.
- The request-policy baseline is nginx 1.29.7 image digest
  `sha256:1854da86e82d5dfb49a8f3d78b099adcc7e36608b207146ed95cd47937938a40`.
- normalized routing URI and original forwarded request-target are distinct.
- With a URI in `proxy_pass`, nginx replaces the matching part of its normalized
  URI, not necessarily the raw request-target: repeated slashes and dot segments
  collapse, and `%7E` becomes `~` in the observed pinned vectors.
- A slash-terminated proxied location redirects the no-slash form (`/api` to
  `/api/`) before proxying. Current RUT routing erases that trailing-slash
  distinction; the #259 bounded slice must reject it rather than approximate it.
- A Redirect action alone cannot prove the nginx source-path condition because
  canonical routing collapses `/api` and `/api/`. Public lowering must execute
  an exact raw `req.pathOnly == "/api"` guard, and the bounded H1 serializer must
  independently revalidate the origin-form raw path before emitting bytes.
- `OPTIONS *` and authority-form CONNECT have no canonical origin-form path in
  current RUT, miss the converter's method-omitted `/` route, and fall through to
  unmatched default 200 before complete-buffering preflight. Internal admission
  rejection tests cannot establish production fail-closed behavior. Pin nginx
  location semantics before creating a routing/unmatched-policy capability issue.
- Complete response buffering currently requires downstream HTTP/1.1; accepted
  nginx configuration syntax does not itself exclude HTTP/1.0 clients.
- Redirect authority must use the actual port returned after `bind`/`getsockname`
  (including declared port `0`), not a listener declaration stored in the
  hot-reloaded RouteConfig.
- nginx and RUT path canonicalization and prefix boundaries differ.
- An invalid upstream status prefix is not an immediate nginx malformed-header
  502: pinned nginx 1.29.7 falls back to HTTP/0.9/close-delimited semantics,
  while RUT currently returns `ParseStatus::Error`. #266 tracks this broader
  gap. The completed malformed differential is intentionally limited to a valid
  HTTP/1.1 status line followed by an invalid empty-name header.
- Host, Connection, hop-by-hop fields, and framing cannot be compared by a
  permissive allowlist when declaring a feature `SUPPORTED`.
- nginx is not installed on the host; Docker provides the pinned nginx test
  dependency. The `%7E` success and explicit-close gateway vectors are committed
  and serialised; broader POST and keep-alive vectors remain manual evidence.
- The cross-thread io_uring stall is fixed and covered by a non-skipping
  real-socket regression, but full differential execution remains serial-only.
- The #263 io_uring downstream-TLS truncation is fixed for its bounded ordinary
  bodyless H1 proxy episode, with exact 256 KiB high/low-watermark evidence.
  nginx HTTPS-listener compatibility remains unsupported because converter and
  differential coverage do not exist; the runtime fix alone is not such a claim.
- nginx 1.29.7 uses HTTP/1.1 and omits upstream `Connection` by default. It uses
  a fixed header skip set rather than dynamic `Connection` token normalization:
  nominated ordinary fields, `Proxy-Connection`, and `Trailer` are forwarded.
- An absolute-form client target is normalized to origin-form by nginx 1.29.7;
  the first request-policy slice may reject it but must remain PARTIAL.
- The first #253 behavior increment supports only a bounded strict HTTP/1.1
  final-response/content-length serializer plus one explicit-close, bodyless
  HEAD slice. HTTP/1.0, HTTP/2, keep-alive HEAD, Upgrade, absolute-form,
  interim/no-body statuses, chunking, close-delimited framing, and response
  mutations remain intentional fail-closed `PARTIAL` limits; broader nginx
  response behavior is not claimed.
- The converter emits both bounded policies. One fixed-CL binary POST now has
  generated-RUT differential evidence, but the one-slice size bound and broader
  framing/request/response domains keep this `PARTIAL`, not general body support.
- nginx's default request buffering is observable at backend accept time. The
  #252 body increment must buffer before upstream allocation/connect; reusing
  transparent early-connect streaming would be a semantic mismatch.
- The first buffering slice is bounded by existing connection-buffer capacity;
  larger nginx request bodies remain unsupported until RUT gains a broader
  memory/file-backed body buffering primitive.
- #253 body admission distinguishes locally buffered completion from actual
  upstream upload completion. Response-before-send-CQE ordering is admitted
  only after a positive full-send completion; partial or failed writes remain
  fail closed.
- Pinned nginx consumes one upstream `Connection` field and synthesizes the
  downstream value without dynamically removing token-nominated ordinary
  fields. The bounded RUT serializer matches those tested vectors and rejects
  duplicate Connection fields; broader hop-by-hop response semantics are open.
- A connect failure has no upstream response, so #253 cannot serialize nginx's
  local gateway page. #256 must supply a separate general failure policy; the
  converter must not embed a hidden nginx-only runtime mode.
- Request-policy materialisation and downstream connection intent are separate:
  the converter now emits response `connection: "request"`, preserving explicit
  client close while the rebuilt upstream request remains reusable H1.1.
